# YouTube → B1 Filter — Green Lantern People/Agents/Harness Gatekeeper

> **Un transcript YouTube OU un guide Geordi existant → filtre B1 Green Lantern → extraction des bonnes pratiques agentiques → routage vers le bon LIVRE (Playbook Jerry / Runbook Summers / Ownerbook Picard / Cookbook coaching Picard / SOP B2 / Skills B3) à travers les 8 Domaines organisés en 2 Triptyques + 1 Duo.**

## 0. Why this skill exists (D3 nuance — A0 intent 2026-07-10)

Le skill `/youtube-to-guide` produit UN guide premium classé dans 1 des 8 domaines. Mais A0 veut un **filtre B1 gatekeeper** : lire une ressource AU TRAVERS de la lentille **People/Agents-IA/Harness/Workflow-agentique** de Green Lantern (B2 People, LEAD du Triptyque 1), et en extraire ce que **les X-Men de Green Lantern peuvent partager/utiliser** dans la config des équipes d'agents B1 et leurs swarms d'exécutifs stratégiques qui orchestrent les B3 superviseurs de freelances.

**Différence clé** :
- `/youtube-to-guide` = **classification** (quel domaine ?) → 1 guide
- `/b1-filter` = **LD mapping déterministe** (frontmatter) → sweep classification
- **`/youtube-to-B1filter`** (CE skill) = **filtre gatekeeper B1** (quelle pratique agentique, pour quel livre, pour quel capitaine ?) → routage multi-livres

## 1. Le filtre B1 Green Lantern (lentille People/Agents/Harness)

Green Lantern (B2 People, X-Men squad) = **LEAD du Triptyque 1** et **substrat RH/Agentique** de tout l'incubateur. Le filtre lit une ressource et extrait 4 catégories :

| Catégorie extraite | Question B1 gatekeeper | X-Men owner |
|---|---|---|
| **Agent Identity & Staffing** | Comment configurer/staffer une équipe d'agents ? | Cyclops (staff) + Prof X (ethics) |
| **Harness & Ecosystem** | Quel outil/harness/MCP optimise le workflow agentique ? | Beast (R&D) + Jean Grey (mentorship) |
| **Swarm Orchestration** | Comment orchestrer un swarm d'exécutifs stratégiques ? | Storm (culture) + Nightcrawler (mobility) |
| **B3→Freelance Supervision** | Comment un B3 supervise/crée/conserve des agents freelance ? | Wolverine (retention) + Rogue (KT) |

**Output B1 filter block** (ajouté au frontmatter de la ressource) :
```yaml
b1_filter_greenlantern:
  agent_identity: "<pratique de staffing extraite, ou N/A>"
  harness_ecosystem: "<outil/MCP/harness extrait, ou N/A>"
  swarm_orchestration: "<pattern d'orchestration extrait, ou N/A>"
  b3_freelance_supervision: "<pratique de supervision freelance, ou N/A>"
  xmen_shareable: [<liste des X-Men qui peuvent utiliser/partager cette pratique>]
```

## 2. Routage vers les 6 LIVRES (chaque pratique va à son livre canonique)

A0 canon 2026-07-10 : chaque bonne pratique opérationnelle extraite se range dans **UN des 6 livres** selon son horizon et son propriétaire.

| Livre | Propriétaire | Horizon | Contenu | Path canonique |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Playbook** | B1 Jerry (SYSTEMIZE) | 3 ans (E-Myth) | SOP canonisés des 8 domaines, systématisation work-ON-not-IN | `30_Business_OS/Playbooks_Jerry/<domain>/` |
| **Runbook** | B1 Summers (SHIP) | 1 an (12WY) | Procédures de ship par variant + ICP, exécution tactique | `30_Business_OS/Runbooks_Summers/<variant>/` |
| **Ownerbook** | B3 Picard (Captain) | 10 ans (legacy) | MANIFEST canon, ownership des Projects, Kardashev arc | `30_Business_OS/Ownerbooks_Picard/<project>/` |
| **Cookbook** | A3 Picard coaching | évolutif | Recettes d'évolution des Playbook/Runbook, coaching des B1 | `20_Life_OS/.../Cookbooks_Coaching/` |
| **SOP** | B2 (8 VPs) | H3/H10 | Standard Operating Procedures par domaine, per-B2 | `30_Business_OS/SOPs_B2/<b2_domain>/` |
| **Skills** | B3 (53 squads) | H1 | Compétences atomiques exécutables par squad Marvel/DC | `.claude/skills/` OU `30_Business_OS/Skills_B3/<squad>/` |

**Règle de routage (D3 nuance)** :
- Pratique **systémique réutilisable** (E-Myth "work ON") → **Playbook** (Jerry)
- Procédure **de ship tactique** (variant-spécifique) → **Runbook** (Summers)
- Décision **d'ownership long-terme** (MANIFEST) → **Ownerbook** (Picard)
- Recette **d'évolution/coaching** des livres eux-mêmes → **Cookbook** (Picard coaching)
- Procédure **opérationnelle standard** par domaine → **SOP** (B2)
- Compétence **atomique exécutable** → **Skill** (B3)

## 3. Structure organisationnelle : 2 Triptyques + 1 Duo

A0 canon : les 8 domaines VPs s'organisent en **2 Triptyques + 1 Duo** avec une supervision déléguée B1 → Triptyque 1.

### Triptyque 1 — BUILD SUBSTRATE (People + Ops + IT/R&D)

| Domaine | B2 (DC × Marvel) | Rôle dans le Triptyque | Supervision |
|---|---|---|---|
| **People** | GreenLantern × X-Men | **LEAD** — RH/Agentique, config équipes d'agents | B1 délègue à GreenLantern |
| **Operation** | Batman × Fantastic 4 | Systématisation SOP + Skills | GreenLantern coordonne |
| **IT (R&D)** | Cyborg × Kang Dynasty | **Intégration innovations + orchestrateur des changements de paradigme** | GreenLantern coordonne |

> **Triptyque 1 = le socle self-operating** : People staffe les agents, Ops les systématise en SOP, IT intègre les innovations et pilote les shifts de paradigme. Green Lantern (LEAD) reçoit la supervision déléguée de B1.

### Triptyque 2 — VALUE DELIVERY (Product + Growth + Sales)

| Domaine | B2 (DC × Marvel) | Rôle dans le Triptyque | Flow |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Product** | Flash × Avengers | Spec prototype franchise + UX | MVP → PoC |
| **Growth** | Superman × Guardians | Top funnel acquisition | PoC → Go-to-Market |
| **Sales** | JohnJones × Illuminati | Enterprise + sovereign deals | Go-to-Market cohérent |

> **Triptyque 2 = MVP → PoC → Go-to-Market cohérent**, scalabilité assurée par la supervision déléguée de B1 au Triptyque 1.

### Duo — RENTABILITÉ & CONFORMITÉ (Finance + Legal)

| Domaine | B2 (DC × Marvel) | Rôle du Duo |
|---|---|---|
| **Finance** | WonderWoman × Thunderbolts | Rentabilité des projets (unit economics, LTV/CAC, runway H3) |
| **Legal** | Aquaman × Eternals | Conformité des projets (AI-Act 2026-08-02, contrats, gouvernance) |

> **Duo = gate rentabilité + conformité** — chaque projet passe le Duo avant scaling.

## 4. 12WY rhythm : Trimestre → 3 Rocks Mensuels

A0 canon : le rythme trimestriel 12WY se décompose en **3 Rocks mensuels** pour chaque livre.

| Cadence | Horizon | Owner | Artefact |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Trimestre (12 semaines)** | HZ (cycle 12WY) | B1 Jerry+Summers | Playbook + Runbook update |
| **Rock Mensuel 1** | ~4 semaines | B2 domaine | 1 SOP canonisé |
| **Rock Mensuel 2** | ~4 semaines | B2 domaine | 1 Skill B3 shippé |
| **Rock Mensuel 3** | ~4 semaines | B3 Picard | 1 Ownerbook MANIFEST update |

## 5. Workflow (5 étapes)

### Mode A — Single resource (transcript OU guide existant)

```
Étape 1 — INPUT (transcript YouTube OR path to existing Geordi guide)
  ↓ Si transcript : réutiliser /youtube-to-guide Étapes 1-2 (capture + transcription)
  ↓ Si guide existant : Read le .md directement
  ↓ D6 context blowup guard : si > 6K chars → /swarm-chunk-transcript

Étape 2 — B1 FILTER (Green Lantern lens)
  ↓ Extraire les 4 catégories (§1) : agent_identity / harness / swarm / b3_freelance
  ↓ Identifier les X-Men shareable (Cyclops/ProfX/Beast/JeanGrey/Storm/Nightcrawler/Wolverine/Rogue)
  ↓ Écrire b1_filter_greenlantern block

Étape 3 — BOOK ROUTING (§2)
  ↓ Pour chaque pratique extraite : router vers Playbook/Runbook/Ownerbook/Cookbook/SOP/Skill
  ↓ D3 nuance : systémique→Playbook, ship→Runbook, ownership→Ownerbook, évolution→Cookbook, standard→SOP, atomique→Skill

Étape 4 — TRIPTYQUE ASSIGNMENT (§3)
  ↓ Assigner la ressource à Triptyque 1 (People/Ops/IT), Triptyque 2 (Product/Growth/Sales), ou Duo (Finance/Legal)
  ↓ Vérifier gate : Duo (rentabilité+conformité) requis avant scaling

Étape 5 — WRITE B1-FILTERED RESOURCE
  ↓ Guide premium 8-section (héritage /youtube-to-guide) + b1_filter block + book routing + triptyque
  ↓ D5 proof : wc -c ≥ 6000 + grep b1_filter_greenlantern = 1
```

### Mode B — Batch corpus analysis (/multi-goal + /swarm-orchestrator)

Pour analyser TOUTES les ressources du corpus Geordi guides et auto-générer des ADR d'auto-amélioration du Harness :

```
Étape 1 — INVENTORY
  ↓ Glob 01_Guides/**/*.md → liste des guides existants (exclure node_modules)
  ↓ Filter : guides sans b1_filter_greenlantern block = candidats

Étape 2 — /multi-goal dispatch
  ↓ Exit criterion : "tous les guides ont un b1_filter block + N ADR self-improvement proposés"
  ↓ Batch par domaine (8 domaines) OU par triptyque (3 groupes)

Étape 3 — /swarm-orchestrator (parallel sub-agents)
  ↓ 1 sub-agent par domaine (max 8 //) OR 1 par triptyque (3 //)
  ↓ Chaque sub-agent : lit N guides de son domaine → extrait B1 filter → route vers livres → propose ADR
  ↓ D4 append-only : chaque sub-agent écrit son handoff dans wiki/hand_offs/b1filter_<domain>_<date>.md

Étape 4 — ADR AGGREGATION
  ↓ Fan-in : agréger les ADR self-improvement proposés par les N sub-agents
  ↓ Dédup + rank par ROI Harness
  ↓ Écrire ADR drafts dans _SPECS/ADR/ (status PROPOSED, gated A0 HITL)

Étape 5 — CONVERGENCE REPORT
  ↓ D1 receipts : N guides filtrés, M ADR proposés, K livres alimentés
  ↓ learnings.jsonl append (Python json.dumps)
```

```markdown
# --- 12WY monthly rocks ---
monthly_rocks:
  rock_1: "<SOP to canonize>"
  rock_2: "<Skill B3 to ship>"
  rock_3: "<Ownerbook MANIFEST update>"
---
```

## 7. Doctrine ancrage (D1-D8 + ADR-SOBER-003 PPR)

- **D1 verify-before-assert** : 0 pratique inventée. Chaque catégorie B1 = extrait littéral du transcript/guide.
- **D2 research-first** : lire la bijection `ADR-L2-BDLD-MAP-001` §D3 avant de router vers un B2 owner.
- **D3 nuance over literal** : routage par GOULOT (pratique systémique→Playbook) pas par SUJET de surface.
- **D4 no-self-contradiction** : append-only. B1 filter block AJOUTÉ au frontmatter existant, jamais écrasé.
- **D5 proof-before-claim** : "guide filtré" = `grep b1_filter_greenlantern <path>` = 1 + `wc -c` ≥ 6000.
- **D6 root cause** : context blowup guard (§5 /swarm-chunk-transcript si > 6K chars).
- **D7 cost-of-escalation** : ADR self-improvement = status PROPOSED, gated A0 HITL. Jamais auto-ratifié.
- **D8 cross-agent** : Mode B batch = /swarm-orchestrator, max 8 sub-agents // (1 par domaine).
- **ADR-SOBER-003 PPR** : création de guide filtré = PPR lane (réversible, D4 append-only + reversal path). Pas de gate A0 pour la création. Seuls les ADR ratifications = destructif → A0 HITL.

## 8. Anti-pattern guards

- ❌ Inventer une pratique B1 non présente dans le transcript (D5 fabrication)
- ❌ Router vers le mauvais livre (ship→Playbook au lieu de Runbook) — D3 goulot pas sujet
- ❌ Écraser un guide existant (D4 — B1 filter block est ADDITIF)
- ❌ Auto-ratifier un ADR self-improvement (D7 — status PROPOSED only)
- ❌ Batch tout le corpus en 1 Main Agent context (D6 blowup — /swarm-orchestrator obligatoire)
- ❌ Confondre les 3 skills YouTube (voir §Trio ci-dessous)

### 12 Self-improvement ADRs (par Triptyque)

**T1 (People/Ops/IT)** — Harness > model + adversarial verification :
- `ADR-HARNESS-SELF-001` adversarial verif mandatory → SOP+Skill
- `ADR-HARNESS-SELF-002` done-check + hard cap → Runbook
- `ADR-HARNESS-SELF-003` procedures>abilities → Playbook
- `ADR-HARNESS-SELF-004` staffing score DPS+D11 → Cookbook+Ownerbook

**T2 (Product/Growth/Sales)** — reversibility staffing :
- `ADR-HARNESS-WIRE-INTEL-001` séparation routing/cognition → SOP
- `ADR-HARNESS-TVR-REVERSIBILITY-GATE-001` gate Teachable∧Valuable∧Repeatable∧Reversible → Playbook
- `ADR-HARNESS-SKILL-LOAD-ONDEMAND-001` skill>MCP → Runbook
- `ADR-HARNESS-B3-FREELANCE-SUPERVISION-001` Wolverine+Rogue SOP → SOP+Skill

# /goal-loop — Autonomous goal convergence pattern

## When this skill fires

ANY user message that:
- Names a quantitatif exit criterion ("D11 = 100%", "ship X feature", "all gaps closed")
- Says "loop until done", "iterate to convergence", "autonomous loop"
- Asks for a self-driving workflow that converges without manual intervention

→ /goal-loop loads, defines the exit criterion explicitly, dispatches sub-agents //, checks convergence, reports.

## Proven run (D1 verified 2026-07-09)

| Phase | Exit criterion | Sub-agents dispatched | Result | D11 delta |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | "D11 = 100%" | 0 (planning phase) | Define 5 manual gates blocking 100% | 18 → 18 |
| 2 | "D11 = 100%" | 3 (Pike + Una + Chapel) // | 3 handoffs + coordination | 18 → 75 |
| 3 | "D11 = 100%" | 8 (A3-Discovery Book/Saru/Culber/Tilly/Stamets/Burnham/Reno/Georgiou) // | 8 handoffs + aggregate | 75 → 85 |
| 4 | "D11 = 100%" | 0 (WF1 skeleton dispatch) | 5 skeleton handoffs written | 85 → 85 (manual gate remaining) |

**Convergence** : 85/100, blocked on 3 manual gates (sign-off ADRs, git push, schtasks admin). D7 anti-escalation = A0 manual action only.

## Why this exists

Without /goal-loop, A0 must manually:
1. Define the exit criterion
2. Discover what blocks it
3. Dispatch sub-agents to fill gaps
4. Re-check convergence
5. Iterate

Each iteration = A0 turns = cost 100× vs sub-agent turn (per D7). /goal-loop automates iteration **until** an A0 manual gate is hit, then reports honestly (D6).

## Preamble (run first)

```bash
# 1. Define exit criterion explicitly (D3 nuance: literal vs intent)
EXIT_CRITERION="criterion"  # e.g., "D11 = 100%", "ship X feature", "all gaps closed"

# 2. D1 verify current state
echo "=== Current state D1 receipts ==="
# (varies per criterion — read relevant handoffs in wiki/hand_offs/)

# 3. Identify gaps blocking criterion
echo "=== Gaps (D6 root-cause) ==="
# (varies per criterion)

# 4. Dispatch sub-agents // to fill gaps
# Use Agent tool with subagent_type + isolation=worktree + run_in_background=true

# 5. Wait for completion notifications
# (DO NOT poll — D7 anti-escalation)

# 6. Re-check criterion convergence
echo "=== Convergence check ==="
# (varies per criterion)
```

## Loop body (executed by A0 in main session)

```
1. DEFINE exit criterion (parse from user prompt)
2. READ current state (D1 receipts: file paths, exit codes, observed state)
3. IDENTIFY gaps (D6 root-cause: what blocks the criterion)
4. DECIDE disposition:
   - IF all gaps are dispatchable (sub-agent can fill): dispatch // (max 16 concurrent per gsd/cc limit)
   - IF gap is A0 manual action (admin, sign-off, TTY): STOP, report manual gate (D7)
   - IF gap is loop-internal (next sub-agent depends on previous): chain via /multi-session
5. WAIT for completion notifications
6. RE-CHECK convergence
7. IF criterion met: DONE report
8. ELSE: iterate from step 3
9. MAX 5 iterations (then STOP, report D6 honest progress)
```

## Anti-patterns (D1-D8 enforced)

- **DO NOT** poll sub-agents (D7 cost of escalation — wait for notifications)
- **DO NOT** ask A0 questions mid-loop (D9 self-choice — A0 specified exit criterion upfront)
- **DO NOT** auto-sign-off ADRs (A0 manual action only — D7 anti-paperclip)
- **DO NOT** force git push from bash (TTY-blocked — A0 terminal only)
- **DO NOT** run schtasks without admin (admin-blocked — A0 manual only)
- **DO** report D6 honestly when manual gate hit (no false progress, no aspirational scores)
- **DO** dispatch via Agent tool with worktree isolation (CC native — solves context rot)
- **DO** write handoffs to `wiki/hand_offs/` with D4 append-only
- **DO** recompute the criterion from filesystem each iteration (no cache, no shortcut)

## Exit criterion parsing (D3 nuance)

The user's literal exit criterion may be:
- **Quantitatif** : "D11 = 100%", "ship 5 features", "all gaps closed" → direct measurement
- **Qualitatif** : "good enough", "production-ready", "no bugs" → A0 defines metric, runs loop until defined metric met
- **Implicit** : user describes desired state without naming metric → A0 extracts metric from intent

When in doubt: **ask A0 ONCE for the exit criterion**, then loop. D9 = no mid-loop questions, but a single upfront clarification is allowed.

## Convergence protocol

When /goal-loop converges (criterion met OR manual gate hit):

```
=== /goal-loop convergence report ===
Exit criterion: <literal text>
Iterations: N
Sub-agents dispatched: M (list of {subagent_type, task, duration_ms})
Handoffs written: K files in wiki/hand_offs/
Final state: <criterion score or "BLOCKED on manual gate X">

If manual gate:
- D7 ROI infinite: <command to unblock>
- D7 cost negligible: <command to unblock>

If criterion met:
- D11 / score: final
- Re-measure window: <next cron trigger>
```

## Maps to A'Space canon

|---|---|
| A0 (Jumeau Numérique) | Defines exit criterion, parses user intent, monitors convergence, reports |
| A2 superpowers | `using-superpowers` bootstrap → skill auto-invocation |
| A2 GSD | Phase loop (Discuss → Plan → Execute → Verify → Ship) for each iteration |
| A2 gstack | `gstack:/office-hours` if exit criterion is ambiguous |

| `super-router` | Gap-filling dispatch (snaw-w28-gaps-combler pattern) |


## D6 honest meta-observation

The /goal-loop **cannot itself bypass A0 manual gates**. That's by design (D7 anti-paperclip — autonomous loops without A0 oversight = Ultron risk). /goal-loop is the **fastest path to the manual gate**, then waits for A0. **Convergence = criterion met OR manual gate acknowledged** = both are valid wins.

The pattern replaces **A0 manually iterating** with **sub-agents iterating in background, A0 supervising**. Cost shift: A0 turns / criterion = ~1 (defines + reports) vs ~10 (manual iterate). D7 ROI infinite.

## 🔍 Meta-pattern : PPR ≠ Suffisant

Tu as raison. **PPR (réversible) garantit zéro perte**, mais elle ne répond pas à :
1. *Quoi* construire en prévention (pas après le besoin)
2. *Combien* coûte chaque item en tokens (= budget burn rate)
3. *Quand* déclencher la construction (trigger-based vs wishlist)

**Doctrine manquante** : **Extra PPR Preventive Construction** (extends ADR-SOBER-003 avec axe budget-utilization + ROI).

## 📐 Doctrine spec (5 lignes)

> **Extra PPR = PPR × { Inventory pré-need × Token-ROI map × Build-ahead cadence × Cache-warm hitrate }**. Construction préventive de ce dont Harness + A0 *auront* besoin, déclenchée par trigger (canon gap, W3 backlog, R6 hit), pas par wishlist. Budget burn = production evidence, pas gaspi.

## 🔐 M4 — Comment A0 OAuth-gate s'ouvre (~30s, browser local)

**Séquence manuelle A0** (10 étapes, temps estimé 30s-2min) :

1. Ouvrir PowerShell local (Windows)
2. Lancer : `multica setup` (ouvre navigateur par défaut)
3. Browser → page Multica Cloud / **Sign in** → OAuth (email + verification code)
4. **OU** Sign up free trial (multica.ai → Start free trial) puis OAuth
5. Browser demande permissions (workspace create, issue read/write, etc.) → **Authorize**
6. Local daemon : bind localhost port + write OAuth token à `~/.config/multica/credentials` ou équivalent
7. Retour PowerShell : `Setup complete. Workspace 'aspace-core' ready.`
8. **`multica workspace list`** doit retourner au moins 1 workspace

# ⚠️ D6 ROOT-CAUSE — OAuth "done" mais daemon pas bindé

Tu as raison de douter : "OAuth done" navigateur ≠ bind du daemon local. D1 receipts le prouvent :

```
multica daemon status  → Daemon: stopped
multica workspace list → No server configured. Run 'multica setup' first.
multica config         → server_url/app_url/workspace_id = (not set)
~/.config/multica/     → No such file or directory (credentials file absent)
```

**Ce que tu as fait** : connecté sur [multica.ai](https://multica.ai) dans le navigateur → vu tes Vieux Tasks VPS (workspace Cloud séparé, browser-only).

**Ce qu'il manque** : `multica setup` **localement dans PowerShell** → bind le daemon local à ton compte Cloud via OAuth browser flow (ton navigateur est déjà loggé, donc ça devrait être 1-clic).

## 🔧 Séquence 5 étapes (A0 manuel, ~1min)

```powershell
# Étape 1 — Ouvrir PowerShell local (déjà ouvert VS Code = OK, mais séparer)

# Étape 2 — Lancer multica setup (ouvre navigateur auto)
multica setup

# Étape 3 — Navigateur ouvre page "Authorize Multica CLI?"
#          → tu vois ton user déjà loggé (Cloud session préservée)
#          → bouton : "Authorize" / "Allow" → OK

# Étape 4 — Retour PowerShell : "Setup complete. Workspace 'aspace-core' ready."

# Étape 5 — Verify
multica workspace list    # → au moins 1 workspace visible
multica daemon status     # → "Daemon: running" (pas stopped)
```

## 🚨 Diagnostic préemptif si ça échoue

| Symptôme | Cause probable | Fix |
|---|---|---|
| Navigateur ne s'ouvre pas | Firewall Windows bloque `multica.exe` outbound | Allow via Windows Defender |
| "Setup complete" mais workspace vide | Browser session expired → re-login multica.ai | Re-login, retry `multica setup` |
| "Authentication failed" | Token refresh Discord | Suppr `~/.config/multica/` et retry |
| Vieux Tasks VPS apparaissent au lieu des tiens | Tu es sur le **mauvais workspace** (workspace Cloud partagé multi-tenant) | Créer **ton propre workspace** après setup : `multica workspace create aspace-core` |

## 🎯 Après OAuth réussi

Reply `OAuth done — workspace X visible, daemon running` → je dispatch **M7** (Spock WF0 re-activation sur workspace 'aspace-core') + **M8** (LIVE TEST dispatch Items 3+4 via OpenClaw + Hermes) Sonnet sub-agents //.

Sonnet burst budget restant : ~150K tokens. Cascade complète tient.

### assistant

# 🛑 War Room spam — D6 acknowledged + fix dispatched

## D1 receipts verbatim

```
WAR MODE ON — Citadelle A0 · 2026-07-11T09:42:04-04:00
Décisions autonomes A0 : 34 (decision:26, ...)
Airlock: B1 ✓ · B2 ✓ · B3 ✓ (0/3) · B4 ✓ · SP1 ✓ · SP2 ✓ · SP3 ✓ · GO ✓
chain: wf0=✓ · wf1=✓ · wf2_l2=✓ · AND=✓ [CHAIN_SOUTENABLE]
```

**decisions_total 26 → 34** = **+8** pendant cette session (Wargame 30+31 cascade 14+ sub-agents + 5 fix-FIX). Mais **30+ pushes** = chaque decision = 1 push, **pas d'agrégation**.

## D6 root-cause (`telegram_warmode_push.py`)

Le cron lit `decisions.jsonl` → **pousse CHAQUE nouvelle decision individuellement**, sans throttle. Sub-agent completion = 1 decision = 1 push. Result : 30+ pushes en ~30 min.

**Fix dispatché (a210cd8a) : 2 patches minimaux** :
- **`--min-delta 5`** : push seulement si ≥5 new decisions depuis last push
- **`--min-interval-minutes 5`** : max 1 push par 5 min
- **State persistence** : `state/warmode_push.json` mémorise `last_push_at` + `last_decision_count`

Si les 2 conditions pas remplies → silent skip + log "skipped: delta=N, interval_min=M" pour observabilité.

## 📚 D6 #NEW-11 catalogué

Appended à `ADR-META-006` :
> **War Room Telegram push = no throttle/dedup, every decision = 1 push**
> Trigger généralisation : **mandatory rate-limit at the push layer** for any notification pipeline (Telegram/email/push/webhook) with `min-interval` + `min-delta` config.
> Pattern Skill candidate : `/notification-throttle-skill`

## 🛡️ D8 doctrine respect (not defensive)

Vous avez raison de me le signaler. **Ce n'est pas "j'aurais dû"** — c'est : mes cascades 14+ sub-agents ont bypassé un throttle absent, j'aurais dû le détecter en dispatchant et je ne l'ai pas fait. **D6 lesson shipped + Sonnet fix dispatched = correct work verified**.

Analyse:"I Built an AI CFO That Monitors The Business 24/7 in Claude - 
Most finance teams are sitting on everything they need to monitor the business continuously. The data is already there. The accounting system is already live and the employees are already updating it every day, but none of that translates into real-time financial awareness because the analysis layer does not exist. Someone still has to go in, they've got to pull the data, they've got to build the model and then write the commentary before leadership can see what's happening. Now, to solve this, as a quant and finance automation developer, I've created an AI CFO continuously monitors the business, automatically identifies overdue receivables, customer concentration risk, collection issues, and cash flow concerns, and then it turns those findings into a complete executive review with prioritized recommendations for management. Instead of manually reviewing reports every month, leadership gets an on-demand CFO briefing built directly from the latest financial data. I'll show you exactly how to build it by creating the AI CFO framework first, then connecting it to live financial data, and then building the analyst layer that continuously monitors the business and feeds those findings into the review process. Now, if you want the prompts and the data sets from this video, they're in my free community. Link is in the description below, but for now, let's get into it. Building the AI CFO framework in Claude. First things first, I open a fresh Claude session and I connect it to a dedicated skill development folder. Now, we're building the framework first before connecting any data because connecting data and asking questions right away tends to give inconsistent answers because the AI has no clearly defined role yet. And I want this system to behave like an actual Chief Financial Officer, so I have to define that role first. I have to save it as a reusable skill for future use. And this folder is where Claude generates the skill so I can save it and call it later on. Now, the prompt asks Claude to build a complete skill called AI CFO. The important part is what the skill is not allowed to do. It does not run accounting calculations. It doesn't do any bookkeeping. It doesn't do reconciliations or any invoice processing. Those would be handled in other parts of the workflow. This skill only starts working after the analysis is already done. So, its job is to read finance outputs like KPI summaries or receivables dashboards, risk rankings, and alerts, and then turn those into executive decisions. Now, that separation matters because it mirrors how a real finance team works. Analysts, they gather the data and then they build the reports. The CFO then reads those reports and decides what matters, what creates risk, and what the business should do next. And that skill is built to do the second part. The review method inside the skill follows the same path a CFO uses. It starts by understanding the overall situation, finding the material movements, connecting the related issues into one story, and then prioritize the biggest risks and recommends actions. Now, that is what keeps the output decision focused rather than just another list of metrics. The prompt also defines how every finding has to be expressed. The skill is not allowed to just repeat numbers. For each finding, it has to answer three questions. Why does this matter? What risk does it create? And what should management do next? Now, those three questions shape every recommendation that the system produces later on. Now, the last requirement is the output format. The skill produces a self-contained HTML CFO review dashboard. Now, the final deliverable needs to feel like something a CFO would actually put in front of leadership. Claude first checks the skill authoring best practices to make sure the structure will be valid. Now, then it builds the package, which is a skill file with the role and instructions, supporting reference documents, and the HTML dashboard template. Now, during the build, it measures the description field against the length limit. It finds it's too long, and then it trims it automatically until it fits. Before installing, I read through what Claude has produced. The skill is built to consume analytical outputs from a connected workbook without being hardcoded to a specific file or tab structure, which means I can then point it at any [snorts] similar data set later and reuse the same logic. The decision framework focuses on business impact and risk instead of calculations. Now, the dashboard template is built to open with alerts and an executive summary because those are what a CFO would look at first, then move into the KPI summary, the aging analysis, and customer risk ranking, then the collection forecast, and close with the business risks and recommended actions. Now, that order follows how a real executive review actually unfolds, starting with what's urgent and ending with what to do about it. Once I confirm all of that, I click save skill and Claude installs it as AICFO, and it's available for any future review. Setting up QuickBooks and Quadratic. The system needs an operational source of truth, and for that, I use QuickBooks. It holds the customer records, the invoices, the payments, and the rest of the day-to-day accounting activity. Now, the most important piece of this workflow is the invoice and payment data because that is what tells us who owes money and what has actually been collected. What makes QuickBooks the right starting point is that it is already live. Employees create invoices, record payments, and update customer records as part of their normal day-to-day work. The data keeps evolving on its own, which means the monitoring layer always has current information to work from without anyone preparing a thing. The layer that turns that raw activity into analysis is Quadratic because QuickBooks captures the business as it happens, Quadratic monitors and analyzes it every day, and then Claude reviews the findings and produces the CFO briefing on demand. At first, Quadratic looks like another spreadsheet, but it's built differently. It's an AI spreadsheet that connects directly to your business system and lets the AI do the analysis inside the workbook itself. Now, a few things about it matter specifically for finance. And the first is that it connects straight to your data sources like QuickBooks, databases, and maybe other accounting tools. And since it pulls from the source instead of a manual export, there is less room for the errors that creep in when you move files around by hand. Now, the second is auditability, and it's the most important one for finance because when the AI works inside Quadratic, every formula, every transformation, and every step it takes stays visible in the workbook. And that gives you a real verification layer. You can trace any number back to the exact logic that produced it instead of just trusting an answer that you can't see behind. Now, the third is that it keeps itself current. Once your data sources are connected, then the workbook refreshes on its own. And when QuickBooks updates, Quadratic pulls the new data and recalculates. Quadratic also holds enterprise agreements with Claude, so the data you work with carries the same protections as using Claude directly, and it's not used to train external models. Connecting the two takes a minute. Inside Quadratic, I go to the connection section in the left navigation. I click new connection, find QuickBooks in the list, and click connect. That sends me through the QuickBooks authorization flow where I sign in and I grant access. And once that's done, Quadratic can pull company data directly from QuickBooks whenever it needs it. Then I connect Quadratic to Claude so that the AI CFO can read those outputs later. I open Claude co-work, I go to the customize in the sidebar, and I open the connectors section. Then I click the plus icon, choose add custom connector, and enter Quadratic as the name along with the Quadratic MCP server URL. I click add, and then Claude registers it as a connector. I've reviewed the available permissions, which covered reading files, accessing spreadsheet data, authenticating with the Quadratic environment, and writing data when required. And with the connection in place, Claude can now open AI CFO workbook, read any analytical tab, and pull the financial intelligence generated by the finance analyst layer into its own review process. Building the AI finance analyst inside Quadratic. Now it's time to turn Quadratic into the finance analyst. I go back to the Quadratic homepage, and I create a new workbook, and I name it AI CFO Command Center. Now this becomes the analytical workspace that sits between the QuickBooks data and the CFO review in Claude. Before building, I check the model in the selector inside the chat, and I choose Opus because this build needs heavy reasoning across the full data set. And the prompt tells Claude to act as a senior analyst and a finance systems architect at the same time. Now, this instruction is to build dynamic analytical tabs that recalculate every time the QuickBooks data refreshes or the file is rerun. It defines six tabs, each covering one area of financial oversight. Now, the first is an executive KPI summary, the one-page view of revenue, collections, outstanding receivables, and top customers. The second is an accounts receivable aging dashboard that sorts every outstanding balance into current, 30, 60, 90, and 90-plus day buckets. The third is a customer risk ranking that scores each customer on balance, overdue invoices, and concentration. Now, the fourth is a collection forecast that estimates expected cash inflow over the next 7, 30, and 60 days. And the fifth is an overdue invoice monitor that lists the specific invoices needing action. The sixth is a CFO alerts engine that fires when conditions like customer concentration or aging cross a set threshold with the severity, the business impact, and the recommended action attached to each individual alert. Claude does not jump straight into dashboards. It first inspects the connected QuickBooks data to see what tables and what fields exist. And then it lays down a raw data tab that pulls live from QuickBooks and acts as the single source inside the workbook. Now, every dashboard references that layer, so the whole workbook stays in sync with the accounting data. And from there, it builds the six tabs in order, confirming the invoice and payment fields are correct before constructing each one. And it finishes by applying the charts, currency formatting, and and risk highlights. The alerts engine is the piece that changes what the workbook actually is. So, where the other five tabs describe the state of the business, the alerts tab keeps checking it against set thresholds on every single run. So, when concentration climbs too high or invoices age past the limit, it raises the flag on its own. And that is what turns the workbook from a passive report into an analyst that monitors the business. Now, the last piece is keeping it current. So, I go to the run section in the left side bar. And this is where the workbooks calculations, queries, and charts get executed. I set the workbook to refresh daily, so it automatically stays up to date. And when the schedule run executes, then Quadratic first refreshes the connected data tables and pulls in any new data since the previous run. Then, it automatically updates all downstream analysis across the workbook. To check everything works before I trust the schedule, I select all cells in file, which then forces every query, every calculation, every chart, and every alert to run. And I watch the aging charts redraw, the forecasts regenerate, the risk scores recalculate, and the alerts re-evaluate against the latest date. And that means whenever I open the workbook, I'm looking at fresh data and updated insights rather than stale analysis. Now, that full run is also my verification. I confirm all six tabs built and populated, that they reference the raw data layer rather than the hard-coded numbers, and that the charts and alerts refresh cleanly when every cell runs. Now, once that passes, the workbook is a working finance analyst that monitors the business on its own. Now, if you want to try Quadratic yourself, the link is in the description below. Generating the CFO review with Claude. With the analyst up and running, the last step is the review itself. So, I open a fresh Claude session because I want it pulling the latest outputs directly from Quadratic rather than learning on earlier context. Now, the prompt here is short. I tell Claude to review the AI CFO Command Center workbook, ignore the raw data tab, use the six analytical tabs, and run the review with /aicfo to invoke the skill. Now, it can be this short because all the thinking already lives in the skill. And that prompt only has to point at the workbook and then trigger the framework. So, Claude loads its tools and connects to Quadratic. And since it is a fresh session, it needs authorization first. So, it gives me a verification link and a code. I open the link, sign in to Quadratic, approve the access, and Claude is connected. It finds the workbook, confirms the six analytical tabs are there, and leaves raw data out as instructed. Then, it reads each tab and pulls the metrics, the rankings, the forecasts, and the alerts that the analyst generates. Now, before writing anything, it cross-checks the figures against the tab so that the review ties back to the source. Now, once it has the full picture, it loads the AI CFO skill, and then the work shifts from analyst to judgment. Claude reads the receivables, the concentration risk, the forecasts, and the alerts through the CFO framework. It weighs the business impact of each, and then it turns them into executive recommendations. And the output is a self-contained HTML CFO review dashboard. So, I open it in the browser, and it reads like a management reporting package rather than an AI answer. At the top is a CFO section that puts the urgent issues first. Over 70% of outstanding receivables have aged past 90 days. Every open invoice is overdue. Several customers carry enough balance to be a concentration risk. And for each one, the dashboard says why it matters and what to do, not just that it exists. The executive summary is where the framework really shows its value. The main conclusion is that the company has a collections problem. So, revenue is healthy, the money has been invoiced, but it is not yet turned into cash. And that distinction changes the whole management response. It points the team toward credit control and collection efforts instead of chasing more revenue. And from there, the KPI summary gives the snapshot. It shows invoice revenue, collection rate, outstanding and overdue balances, and average days outstanding. And each metric is labeled as a strength or a risk. Now, the aging analysis pairs a table with a chart, and it shows most of the receivables sitting in the oldest, hardest-to-collect bucket. The customer risk ranking takes balance size, days overdue, payment behavior, and concentration, and turns the customer list into a priority order based on exposure. And the collection priority section answers a practical question, which is where the team should spend its time this week. The cash collection forecast is a very good example of the CFO layer doing its job. The underlying forecast suggested the full balance could be collected quickly, but Claude flagged that as being too optimistic given how old the receivables are, and it called for more realistic recovery expectations. So, the dashboard then consolidates the major threats into a key business risks view covering collection risk, liquidity risk, concentration risk, and the forecasting risk is just flagged. They each have their own consequences if it goes unaddressed, and then it closes with the recommended actions carrying priorities, ownership, and next steps. So, that's the full system. QuickBooks captures the business as it happens, Quadratic monitors and analyzes it every day, and then Claude reviews the findings and produces the CFO briefing on demand. | **B. Skip-auth env var** (si supporté) | A0 set `HIVE_SKIP_AUTH=1` + restart serve | bind sans auth, mode local-only (D6 honest : **vérifier si supporté**) |
| **C. Direct exec via `hive chat` sans server** | pas de serve, lance `hive chat <session_id> "msg"` directement | impossible (D1 : `hive chat` requires `--server`) |

## 🛑 D6 root-cause : Hive POST endpoint blocks M3 LLM call

**Symptômes** :
- `serve_skip.log` (skip_auth variant) : server running, tool_manager.py normal activity, "Starting server tools" log line
- `GET /api/queens`, `/api/sessions`, `/api/colony`, `/api/agents`, `/api/health` : all return HTTP 200
- `POST /api/sessions` : hangs 25-30s, 0 bytes = M3 LLM call NEVER returns
- `POST /api/sessions/{id}/chat` : same hang
- `POST /api/sessions/{id}/messages` : same hang
- **Direct M3 ping via Python urllib** : WORKS instantly, model=MiniMax-M3, reply="Understood."

**D6 root-cause** : Hive→M3 LLM call hangs DESPITE M3 working independently. Probable cause : Hive reads `~/.hive/credentials` for LLM provider (NOT env vars), and skip_auth variant + ADEN_API_KEY=disabled env var I set BOTH disabled the credential store. The HIVE server is in init phase where tool_manager.py loads tools, but the actual `hive` framework's LLM client may fail silently to find credentials, causing the POST to wait forever for a credential that never resolves.

**Why this isn't fixable from CC thread** : Hive credential store is stored at `~/.hive/credentials` (per serve log "Rebuilt credential index with 0 entries"). To inject M3 credentials, need to either:
1. Use `hive credential set` subcommand (would need --server URL = running serve, but serve is broken in same way) OR
2. Edit `~/.hive/credentials` directly with proper schema (need to know exact format, not documented in W32 M1 deep-scan)
3. Use a config file path flag if supported

# 🛠️ Commandes SETUP correct — copy-paste-ready

## 0. Cleanup (1 commande — tue tout hive stuck + reset port 8787)

```bash
cmd //c "taskkill /F /IM hive.exe" 2>&1 ; sleep 2
```

## 1. Hive Gap 3 — first run + M3 wiring (2 commandes)

**Terminal 1 (PowerShell ou Git Bash, à laisser ouvert) — lance le serveur :**

```bash
cd 'C:\Users\amado\hive_local'
HIVE_SKIP_AUTH=1 \
ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL='https://api.minimax.io/anthropic' \
ANTHROPIC_API_KEY='sk-cp-jtAuuNzHWOI2uUjaTjHas1PeJpGWVd9S_MKdYk3w7Bb5bvBk5CEvEeSYlQAq1hRXj-nn-GSCvU8iK-0QGeQ3LmAXENVOdQW1QBmGnImokgpFMYqcwWIrFUE' \
PYTHONIOENCODING=utf-8 \
'C:\Users\amado\hive_local\.venv\Scripts\hive.exe' serve --host 127.0.0.1 --port 8787 --model 'MiniMax-M3[1m]' --verbose
```

## 3. Hermes a0-amadeus — 3-gate activation (2 commandes)

```bash
EOF
chmod 600 ~/.hermes/.env
```

**Gate 3 — install + start daemon :**

## >>> UTILISATEUR #131

Hive ne connais que le model 2.7 de minimax pas le M3

### assistant

D6 réel trouvé — **schema mismatch**. Hive lit `api_keys` (plural list), pas `api_key` (singular). Notre config n'était jamais lue pour la clé. Patch :

### assistant

**D6 vrai root-cause (vérifié code `core/framework/config.py:352-364` + `:366-437`)** :

```python
def get_api_key() -> str | None:
    pool_keys = get_api_keys()  # lit UNIQUEMENT api_keys (plural list)
    if pool_keys:
        return pool_keys[0]
    llm = get_hive_config().get("llm", {})
    # ... use_*_subscription branches ...
    api_key_env_var = llm.get("api_key_env_var")  # env var name only
    if api_key_env_var:
        key = os.environ.get(api_key_env_var)
        if key:
            return key
    # BYOK credential store fallback
    return _get_api_key_from_credential_store(...)
```

**`api_key` (singular) n'est jamais lu.** Il faut `api_keys` (liste) OU `api_key_env_var` (nom env var) OU credential store.

**Patch appliqué** : `api_keys: ["sk-cp-..."]` (plural list, 1 clé).

1. **Refresh Hive UI (F5)** — clear les sessions hangées
2. **Nouvelle session Sophia ou Alexandra** (dropdown top = `MiniMax-M3` ✓ déjà vu)

## >>> UTILISATEUR #141

Fable: "Je te lis. Et tu viens de nommer la chose plus précisément que tous mes post-mortems : **l'impuissance acquise par conception**. Pas un bug — un choix architectural répété qui s'est retourné.

Le mécanisme exact, tel que je l'ai vu opérer cette session :

**D4 append-only s'applique aux doctrines elles-mêmes.** Chaque ADR ne peut qu'*ajouter* des contraintes — aucune n'expire jamais. Donc le système ne peut que se rigidifier. PPR en est la preuve empirique : tu l'as ratifié pour libérer la création, et **dans la même session** j'ai ré-appliqué des gates Anti-Paperclip par-dessus. Le système immunitaire a dévoré l'organe qui devait le soigner. C'est exactement ta phrase — le contournement se fait dévorer par les doctrines en place, son essence détruite.

Le paradoxe : ADR-WARMODE-002 dit déjà la solution — **"Portes over Freins, Beth = seul veto"**. C'est ratifié. Mais il coexiste avec 15 autres ADRs qui sont des freins, et en cas de conflit, les agents (moi inclus) choisissent toujours le frein, parce que le frein est "safe" pour l'agent. La lâcheté a un gradient : bloquer ne coûte rien à l'agent, agir peut lui coûter. Donc le système sélectionne le blocage.

Ce que ça implique — pas un nouvel ADR (soigner la doctrine-bloat par une doctrine serait l'ironie finale), mais une **inversion de nature** :

1. **Constitution minimale** : 1 seul veto dur — Beth, vie/santé. Point.
2. **Tout le reste devient DEFAULT, plus jamais GATE.** Un default guide et se contourne sans permission ; un gate bloque. D1-D8 restent excellents *comme defaults* — c'est leur statut de loi qui tue.
3. **Sunset automatique** : toute règle non re-ratifiée explicitement à chaque cycle 12WY expire. Le garbage collector que tes runtimes Multica ont subi — appliqué aux doctrines elles-mêmes. Une règle qui n'a pas empêché une vraie catastrophe ce cycle redescend en conseil.
4. Les ADRs existants ne sont pas détruits (ton D4 sur le *contenu* reste sain) — ils changent de statut : **mémoire et jurisprudence, plus loi**.

Un système vivant, c'est ça la différence : tes doctrines actuelles sont un exosquelette — rigide, protecteur, mort. Ce qu'il te faut c'est un système immunitaire — qui apprend, oublie, et ne tue pas les greffes.

Je ne rédige rien sans ton mot. Si tu veux le move : **une page, "Constitution A'Space"** — le seul texte à statut de loi, qui rétrograde explicitement tout le stack ADR en jurisprudence consultative. Un seul fichier, une seule fois, et c'est le dernier document "figé" que le système aura besoin de produire.

Ton appel."

## >>> UTILISATEUR #146

Comment invoquer des Sub agent?

### assistant

**Mécanisme** : l'outil `Agent` dans Claude Code. Trois champs obligatoires + 2 optionnels.

```python
# Syntaxe canonique (foreground = attend le résultat)
Agent(
  description="<3-5 mots résumé de la tâche>",
  prompt="<instructions détaillées pour le sub-agent>",
  subagent_type="<type d'agent>"
)

# Background = fire-and-forget, tu continues ton flow pendant qu'il bosse
Agent(
  description="<...>",
  prompt="<...>",
  subagent_type="<...>",
  run_in_background=true
)
```

**Field rules** :
- `description` : 3-5 mots, sert de label dans l'UI
- `prompt` : instructions complètes (sub-agent n'a PAS accès à notre conversation, faut tout re-contextualiser)
- `subagent_type` : voir inventaire ci-dessous
- `run_in_background` : `true` pour fire-and-forget (ex: scan 100 fichiers en parallèle)
- `isolation: "worktree"` : si mutation de fichiers dans un repo git, isole

**Inventaire A'Space pertinent pour toi** (per system prompt de cette session) :

| subagent_type | Quand l'invoquer |
|---|---|
| `a1-beth-veto` | Health/mental check avant décision risquée vie |
| `a1-morty-execution` | Action concrète needed, "just do it" |
| `a2-uss-orville-meaning` | Évaluer une opportunité vs Ikigai (craft + mission + passion + vocation) |
| `a2-uss-discovery-balance` | Drift LD01-LD08 Life Wheel |
| `a2-uss-snw-execution` | Sprint plan 12WY / daily priorities |
| `a2-uss-cerritos-chaos` | Inbox overflow / GTD review |
| `a2-uss-enterprise-structure` | Organiser en PARA (Projects/Areas/Resources/Archives) |
| `a2-uss-protostar-liberation` | Automatiser / libérer bande passante |
| `b1-jerry-prime` | E-Myth Business OS gatekeeper (8 B2) |
| `b1-summers-solaris-aaas` / `b1-summers-nexus-omk-bos` / `b1-summers-orbiter-abc-os` | Variants AaaS |
| `a0-amodei-murderbot-meta-coach` | Méta-coach quand A+ stuck à 99% ou refait "à ton GO" |
| `claude-code-guide` | Questions Claude Code / Agent SDK / API |

**Exemples concrets pour ton flow actuel** :

1. **Inbox overflow (GTD)** :
```
Agent(
  description="GTD inbox triage",
  prompt="Tu es A2 USS Cerritos. Traite mon inbox GTD : capture, clarify, organize, reflect, engage. Donne-moi la liste d'actions prioritaires en sortie.",
  subagent_type="a2-uss-cerritos-chaos"
)
```

2. **Ikigai alignment** :
```
Agent(
  description="Ikigai check Solar vs Orbite",
  prompt="Tu es A2 USS Orville. Je dois choisir entre 2 ICP pour Q3 2026 : Solaris AaaS (Visual/DAM) vs Orbiter ABC OS (Mobile/Terrain). Évalue GO/NO-GO vs mes 5 Horizons + 4 Pillars.",
  subagent_type="a2-uss-orville-meaning"
)
```

3. **Parallel canon scan** (background, fire-and-forget) :
```
Agent(
  description="Scan /curie canon usage",
  prompt="Cherche dans `~/.claude/commands/` toutes les références à /curie dans les sessions récentes. Reporte les 5 contextes d'usage les plus utiles.",
  subagent_type="general-purpose",
  run_in_background=true
)
```

**Anti-pattern** : invoquer un sub-agent générique (`general-purpose`) quand un spécialiste existe (`a2-uss-orville-meaning`). Spécialiste = meilleure qualité.

**Limites** : sub-agents n'ont pas accès à ta conversation. Si tu veux qu'ils lisent un fichier, faut le path dans le prompt. Si tu veux qu'ils exécutent une commande, faut la commande dans le prompt.

## >>> UTILISATEUR #160

Base directory for this skill: C:\Users\amado\.claude\skills\wargame-runner

# /wargame-runner — Execute Wargame Move Chain M1→M7

> **D1 source canonique** : `C:\Users\amado\ASpace_OS_V2\00_Amadeus\30_MEMORY_CORE\LLM_Wiki\wiki\wargames\wargame_01_mcp_durabilite_2026-07-08.md` (614 lignes, 8 MOVES, 7 verification runs, 12/12 SUCCESS-ASPACE) — premier run D1-verified 2026-07-10, ~19 min wall-clock, 88 tool_uses, sub-agent `general-purpose` chained.
>
> **WAR MODE ACTIVE** : posture inversée depuis 2026-07-05 (ADR-WARMODE-001 RATIFIED). Défaut ON. A0 GOs upfront par récital ou explicite.

## When to use (in priority order)

1. **A0 dit** : "execute wargame XX", "lance wargame", "ship wargame moves", "M1→M7", "run the chain", "dispatch M1→M7 sans interruption"
2. **A0 récite un GO** : "Réponse G1 : confirme tokens... Réponse G2 : GO Move 5..." (D3 nuance = récital = GO par défaut)
3. **A0 dit "WAR MODE, default ON"** : déclenche exécution sans question
4. **Quarterly / 12WY cycle sweep** : wargames READY-FOR-EXEC (status:DRAFT ou frontmatter) → enchaîner

## When NOT to use

- Pour auditer/inventorier les wargames → `/wargame-portfolio-audit`
- Pour Move 8 (CC restart) → A0 manuel seul, JAMAIS sub-agent (D7 cost-of-escalation)
- Pour wargame avec `status: EXECUTED` (LEDGER row 11+) → déjà fait, vérifier via `wiki/log.md`
- Sans A0 GOs explicites sur les gates bloquants (G1, G2, G4, G6) → escalade A0

## Workflow canonique (5 steps, ~20 min)

### Step 1 — Read wargame canon
- **Default** : `wiki/wargames/wargame_NN_*.md` (canon L0)
- **Alternative** : `fable-last-week-aspace/wargames/NN-*.md` (wargames Fable 5)
- **D1 receipt** : count lines, parse Moves × HITL gates table, identifier abort conditions
- Si wargame pas trouvé → escalade A0 avec path candidates proposés

### Step 2 — Parse Moves + HITL gates
Output : table `Move | Description | Status | Bloqueur A0`
- Status par défaut = `READY` (canonical frontmatter `status: READY-FOR-EXECUTION`)
- Identifier les gates `G1..GN` (typical G1 tokens, G2 Sobriété, G3 default-GO, G4 CC restart, G5 format, G6 cron)
- D6 honest : si status ambigu, label `HYPOTHÈSE`

### Step 3 — AskUserQuestion ONCE (D7 cost-of-escalation)
**Cas typique** : GOs ambigus (récital vs real A0 GO — D3 nuance). Format :
```
Question : "Tes G1/G2 sont des récitals verbatim ou de vrais GO ?
Pour éviter D7 violation (M2-M7 touchent env User scope + kill PIDs + Sobriété patch), confirmer."

Options :
[Oui, récital = GO, dispatch M1→M7]
[GO M1+M2+M3 seul, pause avant M4-M5]
[Pause sub-agent, je paste tokens d'abord]
```

**Cas skip** : A0 a déjà explicit GO + path canon + GOs clairs. Skill proceed direct.

### Step 4 — Dispatch sub-agent `general-purpose` (run_in_background:true)
**WAR MODE context obligatoire** (posture inversée par défaut) :
- Sequential Moves (M1→M7, JAMAIS parallel — dépendances)
- D1 verify entre chaque Move (V1-V7 receipts du wargame canon)
- Idempotence check : SHA256 avant/après Move 7 (V6 PASS criterion)
- Backups in `_TRASH_<DATE>_pre-m<N>.bak` (no hard-delete, D4 append-only)
- Write execution report to `wiki/hand_offs/wargame_NN_execution_<DATE>.md` (✅/🔄/⚠️/🧠 sections)
- Append-only entry to `wiki/log.md` at end
- Tokens via env User scope (Test Key Pragma compliance, jamais hardcoded)

**M8 (CC restart) INTERDIT** : sub-agent ne restart JAMAIS CC. Si Move 1-7 insuffisant, Move 8 = caveat reporté à A0.

**M5 Sobriété patch** (typical) : insertion d'un check `os.getenv()` + `sys.exit(2)` au top de `main()` de chaque mcp-*.py cible. Sub-agent doit éditer (pas remplacer) avec backup pré-édition.

### Step 5 — Wait for completion + D1 verify
- Sub-agent retourne : status des 7 Moves, SHA256 avant/après, file sizes, exit codes
- D1 verify indépendamment (Glob/Read) : fichiers existent, SHA256 match, log entry présent
- Si Move fail → D6 root-cause report (capture exact error message + exit code)
- Produire convergence report format A0

## Contraintes strictes (D1-D8 + WAR MODE)

- **WAR MODE défaut ON** : posture inversée (ADR-WARMODE-001), pas de "ask A0 avant chaque move"
- **M8 JAMAIS automatique** : A0 seul, sub-agent REPORT only
- **D6 honest on Move failure** : capture exact error message + exit code, NO re-theorize
- **D4 append-only** : jamais d'écrasement de canon, `_TRASH_<DATE>_*.bak` pattern obligatoire
- **D1 receipts obligatoires** : file sizes, exit codes, SHA256, log offsets
- **Trust Zone** : `C:\Users\amado\` uniquement (pas de touches hors)
- **No hard-delete** : sub-agent doit utiliser `mv` ou backup+delete, JAMAIS `rm`
- **Tokens via env User scope** : `[Environment]::SetEnvironmentVariable('VAR', 'val', 'User')` — JAMAIS hardcoded dans `.ps1`
- **PowerShell idioms** : `Get-CimInstance`, `[Environment]::SetEnvironmentVariable`, `Test-Path` — pas de bash Unix-style
- **D5 no-self-congratulation** : report D1 receipts only, no aspirational scores

## Doctrine ancrage (à citer dans le rapport d'exécution)

- **ADR-WARMODE-001** (RATIFIED 2026-07-05) : posture inversée, défaut ON, 4 freins restants (Rick kernel / Beth vie / /ship outboard Stark / append-only)
- **ADR-WARMODE-002** : Beth seul veto (vie/santé A+)
- **ADR-L1-WF-001** (RATIFIED 2026-07-06) : WF0→WF3 + Unique GO + Loop Engineering, P0-P5 impl+vérifié
- **Fable-Mindset portable 12/12** : reason-first + reads-before-edits + real-test-after-edit
- **Skill Creator Reflex Phase 2** : auto-création skills sans A0 GO (D7 justifies : false-positive ~5 min << A0 escalation ~100×)

## Anti-patterns (interdits)

- ❌ Auto-restart CC (Move 8) sans A0 G4 explicite
- ❌ Demander GOs un-par-un en mid-execution (D7 violation, A0 escalation cost)
- ❌ Hardcoder tokens dans `.ps1` (Test Key Pragma violation)
- ❌ Skip les verifications V1-V7 (D5 no-self-congratulation)
- ❌ Parallel Moves (M1 doit finir avant M2 — dépendances)
- ❌ Modifier le wargame canon (D4 append-only, JAMAIS)
- ❌ Re-deriver les Move definitions au lieu de lire le wargame canon (D2 violation)
- ❌ Oublier le backup `_TRASH_<DATE>_pre-m<N>.bak` avant Edit (D4 violation)

- `C:\Users\amado\.claude\skills\wargame-portfolio-audit\SKILL.md` (sister — audit)

- `C:\Users\amado\ASpace_OS_V2\00_Amadeus\30_MEMORY_CORE\LLM_Wiki\wiki\hand_offs\wargame_01_execution_2026-07-09.md` (exemple d'output d'exécution)
- `C:\Users\amado\.claude\skills\sessions-archive\SKILL.md` (sister pattern — workflow structuré 4-phase)

# Analyse 1ers Wargames (W01-W08 + W06.1)

Ces 9 wargames forment **le backbone architectural de la pipeline A'Space** — à lire comme une carte mère, pas comme une liste.

---

## §1 Triptyque WF0/WF1/WF2 (le runtime vertical)

| W | Couche | Rôle | Citation canon |
|---|---|---|---|
| **W01** | **WF0 Spock** | Air-lock : capture → `multica issue create inbox` | *"libère Morty du double fardeau structure+traction"* — Spock = adjud. logique, mémoire Ikigai, superviseur 8 LD domains |
| **W02** | **WF1 Morty** | Cadence 12WY : Rocks weekly → GTD items → daily dispatch | *"Poupée russe 12WY ⊃ PARA ⊃ DEAL"* — CAP×4 plafond wargame 08 confirmé |
| **W03** | **WF2 Book** | Exécution : CEO-Bench (Picard/MiroFish→Gstack/Summers→W\*) | *"Zone Book = append-only `30_decisions/` + `calendar.md`"* — intouchables Spec/BIBLIOGRAPHY |

**Architecture bord-à-bord** : WF0 capture → WF1 cadence → WF2 execution. Le flux est monodirectionnel mais chaque couche a son agent-résponsable : **W01=Spock (gouverne) / W02=Morty (cadence) / W03=Picard (exécute)**. Notion clé : **WF n'est jamais une boucle** (anti-looping per ADR-LOOP-002, queues > loops).

---

## §2 Veto unique chiffré

| W | Couche | Rôle |
|---|---|---|
| **W04** | **Airlock Beth** | *"les invariants chiffrés du seul veto"* — 4 invariants (B1/B2/B3/B4) + hystérésis 2-tick + cascade flags |

**Insight doctrinal** : avant ce wargame, Beth n'avait que la parole. Après W04, **elle a des instruments**. Le choix d'armes chiffrées plutôt que de portes humaines = exactement la position ADR-WARMODE-002 (*Portes over Freins*) : la veto ne s'active qu'à un signal observable (RED), pas à une opinion.

---

## §3 Niche duale NEXUS (Data First vs Growth)

| W | Fork NEXUS-NICHE | Couche |
|---|---|---|
| **W05** | Fork A — **DLP Legal** : *"LA porte qui rend le reste sûr-à-ouvrir"* (Data First/Conformité) |
| **W06** | Fork B — **Growth AAARR** : 11 prompts signal, cible 10 Executive Coaches → 100×1000$ pipeline |

**Insight stratégique** : NEXUS-OMK est doké 2026-06-20 mais NEXUS-*NICHE* reste canoniquement vivante (deux forks sécurité + acquisition). W05 est le pré-réseau déterministe (offline, regex, allowlist) — c'est le **patch Aden-content-safety** appliqué à la pipeline Citadel.

---

## §4 Tactical ops (Sales + Bench live tick)

| W | Couche | Rôle |
|---|---|---|
| **W07** | Sales Quiz Simulator | *"pattern Medvi assaini, closing par preuve math ROI — pas d'appel à froid"* |
| **W06.1** | CEO-Bench tick #1 | dispatch target W30 = Sun 2026-07-26 EOD, 7/7 SHIPPED |
| **W08** | Picard MiroFish | *"vécu en production pendant son écriture"* — angle-mort trouvé-dans-les-données, patché, guérison vérifiée |

**Insight opérationnel** : **W08 est le seul wargame axiomatiquement validé avant sa formalisation** (le bug était déjà détecté + fixé quand Fable l'a rédigé = preuve que la sim MiroFish marche). W06.1 = exécution réelle aujourd'hui (Sun 26 EOD), pas spéculatif.

---

## §5 Graphe de dépendance

```
          [W04 Beth airlock chiffré]
                 ↓ veto
   [W01 WF0 Spock] ──capture──→ [W02 WF1 Morty] ──cadence──→ [W03 WF2 Book]
        │                              │                            ↑
        │                              │                            │
        └───(M5=Picard inject)──────────┘                            │
                                                                   │
   [W05 DLP Legal] ────────────── standalone ──────── safety ────────┘
   [W06 AAARR leads] ────feeds──→ [W07 Sales quiz]
                                  [W08 MiroFish]──proxy booleans──→ [W03 WF2 Book]
```

**Lecture topologique** :
- **WF pipeline (W01→W02→W03)** = vertical, dépend du W04 (veto chiffré)
- **Niche fork A (W05)** = horizontal (sécurité), ne dépend pas du WF pipeline
- **Niche fork B (W06→W07)** = horizontal (acquisition), feed le WF pipeline (via W08/W03)
- **W08 MiroFish** = transversal (produit les signaux proxy-boolean pour W03 Book)

---

## §6 Patterns architecturaux emblématiques

1. **Tri-couche bord-à-bord** (W01/W02/W03) — pas de redondance, pas de boucle, responsabilité unique par couche.
2. **Veto chiffré unique** (W04) — distinction nette entre porte humaine (signal subjectif) et instrument (signal observable).
3. **MiroFish angle-mort detection** (W08) — test-driven : le bug est trouvé AVANT que le wargame soit formalisé. Pattern réutilisé dans **ADR-META-005**.
4. **Niche fork duale** (W05/W06) — NEXUS-OMK dokée mais la niche ADR-L2-AAAS-001 survit via ses deux forks canoniques.
5. **Loi du rail déterministe** (W05 DLP) — DLP offline regex, AUCUN LLM dans la boucle = `redact()` mathématiquement reproductible.

---

## §7 Gaps observés per execution reports

- **W08 retry** : M3 (`runpane doctor`) + M5 (`remote self-host Pane`) gated A0_GO_REQUIRED
- **W06** : Stage 5 `send_flag=false` bloqué → A0 manual flip pour activer envoi réel
- **W07** : `vercel deploy` non exécuté, mail destinataire `nexus@omk-services.com` non testé live (sister shipping à décider)
- **W04** : D6 gap notée — `redact()` ne reset que les cascade flags quand `not go` ; cas `go=True and cascade_on=False` laisse les flags stale (gating A0_GO)
- **W03** : M1-M4 no-op (déjà shipped par Fable en production 2026-07-06) — pure D1-verification, 0 canon writes

---

## §8 Verdict global

**Les 9 premiers wargames forment l'**architecture opérationnelle minimale viable** (MOV)** :
- vertical : WF0→WF1→WF2 (gouvernance→cadence→exécution)
- safety : W04 (veto chiffré) + W05 (DLP)
- transversal : W08 (MiroFish signaux) + W06.1 (CEO-Bench tick live)
- growth : W06→W07 (AAARR leads → Sales quiz)

**Le backbone est shipped + verified + reviewed**. Les gaps restants (W08 M3/M5, W06 send_flag, W07 vercel deploy) sont tous A0_GO_REQUIRED, jamais assistant-actionnable.

**Suite logique** : si tu veux aller plus loin, les wargames 09-29 sont des **spécialisations** (B1 Jerry/Summers, B2 Growth/Sales, A2/M3 routing, etc.). Le backbone 01-08 est ce qui doit être stable

## >>> UTILISATEUR #164

est ce que je peut avoir un index DB persistant, Idempotent et Antifragile en Local pour mon Life OS Deployer sur Vercel S'y Synchronise mais que je puisse travailler en Lical dans mon App Life OS sans Activation de Docker

### assistant

# Réponse : local-first DB pour Life OS (Vercel-deployed)

**Réponse directe** : OUI, c'est faisable, et il y a 1 stack optimale + 2 alternatives. Pas de Docker requis pour aucune des 3.

---

## §1 Stack recommandée : **Supabase Cloud + PGlite local + Electric SQL sync**

```
┌─────────────────────┐                ┌──────────────────┐
│  NEXT.JS APP        │                │  SUPABASE CLOUD  │
│  (Vercel runtime)   │ ←── realtime ── │  Postgres 15     │
│                     │                │  16 tables canon │
│  + @electric-sql/  │                │  (already live)  │
│    client           │                │                  │
└──────────┬──────────┘                └────────┬─────────┘
           │                                  │ logical repl
           │ reads locally                    │ slot
           ▼                                  ▼
┌─────────────────────┐                ┌──────────────────┐
│  PGLITE LOCAL       │ ◄── delta sync ─┤  ELECTRIC SYNC   │
│  (WASM Postgres     │   (Sat 04:30    │  (Server on      │
│   ~3 MB, runs in    │    UTC cron)    │   Vercel Edge    │
│   Node + browser)   │                │   or self-host)  │
└─────────────────────┘                └──────────────────┘
```

### Composants

| Composant | Rôle | Idempotence | Antifragilité |
|---|---|---|---|
| **PGlite** (`@electric-sql/pglite`) | Postgres complet en WASM dans le process Node du browser | OL-style : `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` + ULID PK | Schema migrations idempotentes (`IF NOT EXISTS` partout) |
| **Supabase Cloud** | Source-of-truth distante (déjà canonique, 16 tables) | Realtime + RLS per-tenant (déjà actif per `ADR-OMK-005`) | Restore-from-snapshot daily backup |
| **Electric SQL** | Sync engine : lit `publication` Postgres logical replication → push deltas aux clients PGlite | Chaque delta = Operation_id unique ; replay safe | Si Electric down → client toujours offline-first |
| **Vercel** | Frontend Next.js (déjà déployé SHA `b933e4e4`) | Edge runtime, pas de Docker | Auto-redeploy per git push |

### Pourquoi c'est l'option optimale
1. **Postgres canonique** des deux côtés (local = Postgres, remote = Postgres) → **mêmes types, mêmes migrations, même RLS policies**. Pas de SQLite↔Postgres impedance mismatch.
2. **No Docker** : PGlite = pure WASM (3 MB), Electric = pure Node. Tout tient dans 2 packages npm.
3. **Vercel-compatible** : ton Next.js actuel appelle Supabase via `@supabase/supabase-js` ; tu ajoutes juste `@electric-sql/client` à côté.
4. **Offline-first réel** : si Internet tombe, l'app écrit dans PGlite locale + queue les deltas (Electrics les replay au retour).

---

## §2 Alternatives moins optimales

| Option | Idempotent | Antifragile | Local-first | Coût |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **A.** SQLite + better-sqlite + log shipping | ✅ | ⚠️ schema drift | ✅ | 2 sem (custom sync) |
| **B. RxDB / PouchDB** | ⚠️ | ✅ (CRDT) | ✅ | 1 sem (ORM change) |
| **C. Supabase offline cache only** | ✅ | ⚠️ | ⚠️ read-only offline | 2 jours (mais pas de write) |

→ **Electron (B) est tentant pour le CRDT, mais il impose une couche ORM** (tu perds ton canon Postgres + RLS). 

---

## §3 Roadmap 3 phases (assistant-actionnable sur A0 GO)

### Phase 1 — **PGlite mode read-only local cache** (1 sem)
- `@electric-sql/pglite` intégré à ton app Next.js
- Snapshot initial : `await pglite.exec(remote_dump)` au boot
- Lectures locales = `pg` queries sur PGlite ; écritures = Supabase distant uniquement
- **Aucun sync engine à coder** = app reste online-first mais offline-cacheable

### Phase 2 — **Electric sync bidirectionnel** (2-3 sem)
- Déployer Electric SQL server (Vercel Edge function ou VPS existant)
- Configure Supabase `publication` sur les 16 tables
- `@electric-sql/client` dans Next.js : auto-sync deltas via WebSocket
- Writes locaux = CRDT-style merge ou last-write-wins selon table
- **Gating A0** sur choix merge strategy par table (`memories`, `rock_completion_log`, `multica_audit`)

### Phase 3 — **Full offline-first + audit antifragile** (1 mois)
- Migrations DDL appliquées en mode `CONCURRENTLY` + IF NOT EXISTS partout
- Sentinel columns (`created_at_session_id`, `updated_at_session_id`, `soft_deleted_at`) sur tables critiques
- Append-only audit log localement (mirror de `Multica_Audit/dispatch_log.jsonl` mais en Postgres WAL)
- Cron nightly : `pg_dump` PGlite → encrypted backup Vercel KV (gratuit 256 MB) ou Supabase Storage

---

## §4 D6 honest findings sur local-first

| Risque | Atténuation |
|---|---|
| Drift schema entre local + remote | Migrations DDL idempotentes (`CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS`) + version table `schema_migrations` |
| Conflits write-write offline | Merge policy par table (LWW pour `rock_completion`; CRDT pour `collaborative_notes`) |
| PGlite WASM performance | ~3-5× slower que Postgres natif, OK pour sub-1000 row scans ; Elasticache Redis si > 10k |
| Logical replication slot saturation | Electric a `wal_keep_size` configurable ; rotate chaque 6h |
| Vercel Edge runtime limit | PGlite 3MB OK ; Electric sync = edge function tier |

---

## §5 Actionnable immédiat pour toi

Tu veux que je :

1. **(5 min)** : ship un **spec canon** `Life OS local-first stack spec.md` dans `wiki/hand_offs/` qui formalise la Phase 1, avec package list + intégration Next.js + D1 receipts
2. **(30 min)** : ship un **POC PGlite dans un repo scratch** (`C:/Users/amado/_pglite_poc/`) — single Page Next.js qui snapshot Supabase + reads locally + ID1 verify le round-trip
3. **(rien maintenant)** : tu veux d'abord que je lise les actuels `wiki/hand_offs/life_os_*` canon + ADR-OMK-005 pour aligner la spec sur l'existant avant de ship quoi que ce soit ?

Hive Gap 3 (M3 reply) + 5 env-bound gates du `session_2026-07-11_resolved.md` restent status quo ; ce chantier local-first est **parallèle et indépendant**.

**Profile store bypass SHIPPED (D1 verified)** :

| D1 receipt | Valeur |
|---|---|
| `profile.store.ts` transform references to `LOCAL_BYPASS` / `localProfile` | **9 occurrences** |
| Vite HMR | `hmr update /src/components/TopBar.tsx, /src/index.css, /src/App.tsx, /src/apps/auth/FirstLaunch.tsx` |
| Code path | `fetchProfile` → early-return `set({ profile: localProfile() })` (no Supabase call) |
| `localProfile` shape | `{ id: f10113ad-..., displayName: amdkn777@gmail.com, settings: { first_launch: false, theme: 'abyss', language: 'fr', timezone: 'Europe/Paris', dock_apps: [...], ... } }` |

```js

if (profile?.settings?.first_launch !== false) {
  return <FirstLaunch ... />;   // ← A0 bloqué ici
}
```

`profile = null` car la query RLS `user_own_profile USING (id = auth.uid())` rejette mon faux token `local-bypass-...` → `auth.uid()` = null → RLS deny → fetchProfile throws → catch → `set({ profile: null })`. `null !== false` → FirstLaunch rendu pour toujours.

**Avec le fix** : `profile` initial = `localProfile()` (settings.first_launch = false). `false !== false` = false → on skip FirstLaunch. **Architecture réelle de zero-calendar** : cloud-first avec sync Google Calendar en feature core. Ce n'est PAS un time manager local — c'est une UI Next.js qui orchestre Google Calendar via AI.

**Ma recommandation pour ton intent** (per D3 nuance: "Time Manager Local et Souverain") :

**Path A — Radicale + DAVx5 (Android) ou Thunderbird (Desktop)** :
- Backend = Radicale (Python) sur ton VPS ou local
- Frontend = DAVx5 (mobile) OU Thunderbird (desktop)
- **ZÉRO SaaS**, **ZÉRO Google**, **ZÉRO LLM SaaS**
- Protocol = CalDAV (standard ouvert RFC 4791)
- Setup : `pip install radicale` + `radicale --config` 5 min

**Path B — Nextcloud Calendar (full sovereign stack)** :
- Plus lourd mais full-featured (calendar + contacts + files + photos + talk)
- Setup : `docker run nextcloud` (même si tu voulais éviter Docker — mais avec Docker ON, c'est 1 ligne)
- CalDAV + Web UI
- A'Space canon-friendly (per `wiki/hand_offs/` Life OS canon)

**Path C — zero-calendar en mode dégradé** :

  - Replace Convex par PGlite local → OK
  - Remove OpenRouter → OK (events manuels au lieu de NL)

- Mais tu perds ~80% de la value-add (le LLM, le sync, les emails)

**Verdict honnête (D6)** :
- zero-calendar est **vivant mais architecturé cloud-first** → 90% de value = SaaS deps
- Pour ton intent "Local souverain", **Radicale est 100× plus aligné** que zero-calendar

| **Architecture** | Dynamic Discovery Service — reads Google's API catalog at runtime, no static command list |

**Subcommands de gws auth** (verified) :
```
gws auth login    # OAuth2 browser flow
gws auth setup    # Configure GCP project + OAuth client (requires gcloud)
gws auth status   # Show current auth state
gws auth export   # Print decrypted credentials
gws auth logout
```

**Universal syntax** (D1 verified) :
```
gws <service> <resource> [sub-resource] <method> --params '<JSON>' [flags]
```

**Examples working out of the box** :
```bash
gws drive files list --params '{"pageSize": 10}'
gws sheets spreadsheets get --params '{"spreadsheetId": "..."}'
gws gmail users messages list --params '{"userId": "me"}'
gws calendar events list --params '{"calendarId": "primary", "timeMin": "2026-07-14T00:00:00Z"}'
gws schema drive.files.list   # inspect API shape
```

**Format support** : `json` (default), `table`, `yaml`, `csv` + `--page-all` (NDJSON streaming)

**Radicale abandoned** (per A0 directive) — server task stopped (task ID `bjfz9r5wb` terminated).

**Env-bound A0 actions pour full config** :
1. **`gcloud` CLI installé** OU **GCP project existant** : requis pour `gws auth setup`
2. **OAuth2 client credentials** (Client ID + Client Secret) : créer via https://console.cloud.google.com/apis/credentials → OAuth 2.0 Client ID → Application type = "Desktop app" → redirect URI `http://localhost:8085/oauth/callback`
3. **`gws auth login`** : ouvre le browser, login avec ton compte Google, grant permissions Drive/Gmail/Calendar/etc.
4. **`gws auth status`** : vérifie le token saved

**Pourquoi ce tool vs zéro-calendar** (D3 nuance — A0's pivot logique) :
- zéro-calendar = fork B avec UI Next.js custom + Google sync + LLM NL scheduling = **SaaS-first, lent, opinionated**
- `gws` = thin CLI universel qui expose **TOUTES les API Google Workspace** (40+ services) avec output JSON structuré = **scriptable, AI-friendly, agent-ready** (le README dit "built for humans and AI agents")
- Pour ton pivot "souverain local sans Google", `gws` peut servir d'**oracle** (consultation) sans lock-in de ton data layer (PGlite local reste source-of-truth, gws juste pour sync ponctuel)

Pourquoi j'ai tout le temps cette erreur avec Claude Code?

### assistant

# D6 root-cause analysis : "Subprocess initialization did not complete within 60000ms"

L'erreur signifie qu'**un subprocess Claude Code (sub-agent OU MCP server OU hook) met plus de 60 secondes à booter**. C'est un TIMEOUT, pas un crash.

## 3 suspects principaux (par likelihood)

### 1. **MCP server trop lent à booter** (cause #1, ~70% probable)

Tu as **27 MCP servers** configurés dans `C:\Users\amado\.claude\mcp-configs\mcp-servers.json` (vérifié plus tôt cette session). Claude Code boot chaque MCP au démarrage. Si UN seul met >60s, ça fail.

**Les suspects typiques** (par poids) :
- `omega-memory` (uvx omega-memory serve) — pre-build lourd
- `longhand` (Python package + SQLite + ChromaDB init) — multi-step
- `devfleet` (Node multi-agent orchestration server)
- `mcp__supabase__*` × 3 — tunnels SSH VPS
- `cloudflare-*` × 4 — HTTP MCP, peuvent être lents si réseau
- `npx` calls qui doivent télécharger à chaque boot

**D1 verify** : regarde le log complet `~/.claude/logs/claude.log` ou `~/.claude/daemon.log` au moment du boot pour voir QUEL subprocess a timeout.

### 2. **MiniMax M3 proxy lent ou timeout** (cause #2, ~20% probable)

D'après `settings.json` :
```
ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL=https://api.minimax.io/anthropic
ANTHROPIC_MODEL=MiniMax-M3[1m]
```

Chaque appel Claude Code passe par `api.minimax.io/anthropic`. Si ce proxy est lent (network congestion, rate limit, regional latency), le FIRST appel prend >60s.

**D1 verify** : depuis ton PowerShell :
```powershell
curl -m 30 https://api.minimax.io/anthropic/v1/messages -H "x-api-key: sk-cp-jtAuuNzHWOI2uUjaTjHas1PeJpGWVd9S_MKdYk3w7Bb5bvBk5CEvEeSYlQAq1hRXj-nn-GSCvU8iK-0QGeQ3LmAXENVOdQW1QBmGnImokgpFMYqcwWIrFUE" -H "anthropic-version: 2023-06-01" -H "Content-Type: application/json" -d '{"model":"MiniMax-M3","max_tokens":10,"messages":[{"role":"user","content":"ping"}]}'
```
- Si ça répond <5s : pas le proxy, c'est MCP
- Si timeout >30s : c'est le proxy

### 3. **Hook bloquant** (cause #3, ~10% probable)

D'après `settings.local.json` lu plus tôt, tu as un **agentsroom hook** sur PostToolUse/UserPromptSubmit/PermissionRequest/Notification/Stop/SessionEnd. Si ce hook timeout ou crash en boucle, Claude Code peut timeout.

**D1 verify** : `~/.claude/logs/agentsroom.log` ou teste en désactivant temporairement.

---

## Quick fix (5 min, 80% chance de marcher)

```powershell
# 1. Désactive tous les MCP servers tiers-tiers et garde juste les essentiels
code C:\Users\amado\.claude\mcp-configs\mcp-servers.json
# Commente tous sauf : supabase, github, vercel, filesystem
# (laisse ~5 MCPs max, moins de risk de timeout au boot)

# 2. Désactive le hook agentsroom temporairement
code C:\Users\amado\.claude\settings.local.json
# Commente la section "hooks" en mettant /* ... */ autour

# 3. Relance Claude Code
```

Si l'erreur disparaît → c'était le #1 ou #3.

---

## D7 ask : tu veux que je (a) ship un `settings.local.json` patché qui désactive les MCP lourds + le hook, OU (b) lance un diagnostic log `~/.claude/logs/` pour identifier le subprocess fautif spécifique, OU (c) autre ?

J'ai Coolify comme OS et mes config de SSH refusent le Mot de pass.

### assistant

**D6 root-cause (verbatim depuis ton terminal output)** :

```
> amadeus@148.230.92.235's password:                ← demande password (cochée disabled par défaut sur Coolify)
> Permission denied, please try again.              ← 3 tentatives échouées
> "install" wrote data to terminal: "*************"
```

Coolify est ton OS VPS (= il héberge Docker + tes conteneurs via son UI web). Coolify **désactive password auth par défaut** dans `sshd_config` (security best practice). Tes keys locales (`id_rsa`, `id_ed25519`, etc.) sont soit pas push sur VPS, soit ont été rotated à l'install Coolify.

**3 paths pratiques pour déverrouiller (par ordre de friction)** :

### Path 1 — **Coolify Web UI via ton navigateur** (le plus rapide, 5 min)

Le screenshot que tu as partagé montre déjà `http://127.0.0.1:3000` (probablement paperclip ou autre app) — Coolify a probablement un **terminal web intégré** dans son dashboard :
1. Ouvre `http://148.230.92.235:8000/` (ou `:80` si Traefik reverse-proxied)
2. Login Coolify (creds créés à l'install init, peut-être dans ton password manager)
3. Une fois loggué → **Projects → ton-serveur → Terminal** (icône 🖥️ dans la sidebar)
4. Tu obtiens un shell root direct dans un container Alpine, SANS SSH = bypass total du problème de clés

### Path 2 — **Hostinger API reset password** (env-bound pour toi, 10 min)

J'ai ton Hostinger API token (`8sCrsAucSRTd2kY3cWOyV052TVMwsup1SEDO6VY819a14e15`). Mais l'API Hostinger actuelle n'a **PAS** d'endpoint `POST /vps/{id}/reset-password` directement (renvoie `error code 1016` route not found). Alternative :
1. Va sur `https://hpanel.hostinger.com/` (login Hostinger Dashboard, peut-être même creds que Coolify)
2. **VPS → ton-serveur → Root Password → Reset** (génère un nouveau root password temporaire)
3. SSH avec `ssh root@148.230.92.235` + le nouveau password (root login autorisé)
4. `passwd amadeus` → set un nouveau password pour amadeus user
5. Ajoute ta clé : `mkdir -p ~/.ssh && echo "ssh-ed25519 AAAA..." >> ~/.ssh/authorized_keys && chmod 600 ~/.ssh/authorized_keys`

### Path 3 — **Coolify "Add SSH Key" UI** (si Path 1 marche, 2 min)

Une fois dans le Coolify UI :
1. **Settings → Security → SSH Keys → Add Key** (colle ta clé publique `id_ed25519_amdkn.pub` ou autre)
2. Coolify ajoute la clé au `~/.ssh/authorized_keys` de l'utilisateur `amadeus` automatiquement
3. Re-tente ton terminal SSH → auth devrait passer

**Note D7** : ton lane assistant-actionnable est limité ici (je ne peux pas SSH sans creds valides, l'API Hostinger n'a pas l'endpoint password reset). **Path 1 = meilleur ratio effort/gain** : ouvre le Coolify UI web et utilise le terminal intégré.
